empty:hidden mb-14" data-page-title="How the agent loop works" data-page-href="/en/agent-sdk/agent-loop" id="content"><span data-as="p">The Agent SDK lets you embed Claude Code’s autonomous agent loop in your own applications. The SDK is a standalone package that gives you programmatic control over tools, permissions, cost limits, and output.</span>
<span data-as="p">Both the TypeScript and Python SDKs bundle a native Claude Code binary, so most installs need no separate Claude Code install. See the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/quickstart" class="link">quickstart’s install note</a> for the installs that do.</span>
<span data-as="p">When you start an agent, the SDK runs the same <a href="/docs/en/how-claude-code-works#the-agentic-loop" class="link">execution loop that powers Claude Code</a>: Claude evaluates your prompt, calls tools to take action, receives the results, and repeats until the task is complete. This page explains what happens inside that loop so you can build, debug, and optimize your agents effectively.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="the-loop-at-a-glance" aria-labelledby="_R_79deehkd74t9_"><a href="#the-loop-at-a-glance" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: The loop at a glance"></a><span id="_R_79deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">The loop at a glance</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">Every agent session follows the same cycle:</span>

<ol>
<li><strong>Receive prompt.</strong> Claude receives your prompt, along with the system prompt, tool definitions, and conversation history. The SDK yields a <a href="#message-types" class="link"><code>SystemMessage</code></a> with subtype <code>&quot;init&quot;</code> containing session metadata.</li>
<li><strong>Evaluate and respond.</strong> Claude evaluates the current state and determines how to proceed. It may respond with text, request one or more tool calls, or both. The SDK yields one or more <a href="#message-types" class="link"><code>AssistantMessage</code></a> objects, one for each content block, such as a text block or a tool call request.</li>
<li><strong>Execute tools.</strong> The SDK runs each requested tool and collects the results. Each set of tool results feeds back to Claude for the next decision. You can use <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/hooks" class="link">hooks</a> to intercept, modify, or block tool calls before they run.</li>
<li><strong>Repeat.</strong> Steps 2 and 3 repeat as a cycle. Claude continues calling tools and processing results until it produces a response with no tool calls.</li>
<li><strong>Return result.</strong> The SDK yields a final <a href="#message-types" class="link"><code>AssistantMessage</code></a> with the text response (no tool calls), followed by a <a href="#message-types" class="link"><code>ResultMessage</code></a> with the final text, token usage, cost, and session ID.</li>
</ol>
<span data-as="p">A quick question such as “what files are here?” might take two or three <a href="#turns-and-messages" class="link">turns</a>: one or two calling <code>Glob</code>, then one responding with the results. A complex task such as “refactor the auth module and update the tests” can chain dozens of tool calls across many turns as Claude adjusts after each result.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="turns-and-messages" aria-labelledby="_R_j9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#turns-and-messages" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Turns and messages"></a><span id="_R_j9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Turns and messages</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">A turn is one pass through the loop: Claude produces output, and if it includes tool calls, the SDK executes them and the results feed back to Claude automatically. This happens without yielding control back to your code. Turns continue until Claude produces output with no tool calls, at which point the loop ends and the final result is delivered.</span>
<span data-as="p">Consider what a full session might look like for the prompt “Fix the failing tests in auth.ts”.</span>
<span data-as="p">First, the SDK sends your prompt to Claude and yields a <a href="#message-types" class="link"><code>SystemMessage</code></a> with the session metadata. Then the loop begins:</span>
<ol>
<li><strong>Turn 1:</strong> Claude calls <code>Bash</code> to run <code>npm test</code>. The SDK yields an <a href="#message-types" class="link"><code>AssistantMessage</code></a> with the tool call, executes the command, then yields a <a href="#message-types" class="link"><code>UserMessage</code></a> with the output (three failures).</li>
<li><strong>Turn 2:</strong> Claude calls <code>Read</code> on <code>auth.ts</code> and <code>auth.test.ts</code>. The SDK yields an <code>AssistantMessage</code> for each call and returns the file contents.</li>
<li><strong>Turn 3:</strong> Claude calls <code>Edit</code> to fix <code>auth.ts</code>, then calls <code>Bash</code> to re-run <code>npm test</code>. All three tests pass. The SDK yields an <code>AssistantMessage</code> for each call.</li>
<li><strong>Final turn:</strong> Claude produces a text-only response with no tool calls: “Fixed the auth bug, all three tests pass now.” The SDK yields a final <code>AssistantMessage</code> with this text, then a <a href="#message-types" class="link"><code>ResultMessage</code></a> with the same text plus cost and usage.</li>
</ol>
<span data-as="p">That was four turns: three with tool calls, one final text-only response.</span>
<span data-as="p">You can cap the loop with <code>max_turns</code> / <code>maxTurns</code>. Count each turn with tool calls, plus one for the final text-only response. Without that extra turn, the run ends with a <code>ResultMessage</code> of subtype <code>error_max_turns</code> and no final response. For example, <code>max_turns=2</code> in the loop above would have stopped before the edit step. You can also use <code>max_budget_usd</code> / <code>maxBudgetUsd</code> to cap turns based on a spend threshold.</span>
<span data-as="p">Without limits, the loop runs until Claude finishes on its own, which is fine for well-scoped tasks but can run long on open-ended prompts (“improve this codebase”). Setting a budget is a good default for production agents. See <a href="#turns-and-budget" class="link">Turns and budget</a> below for the option reference.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="message-types" aria-labelledby="_R_139deehkd74t9_"><a href="#message-types" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Message types"></a><span id="_R_139deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Message types</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">As the loop runs, the SDK yields a stream of messages. Each message carries a type that tells you what stage of the loop it came from. The five core types are:</span>
<ul>
<li>
<span data-as="p"><strong><code>SystemMessage</code>:</strong> session lifecycle events. The <code>subtype</code> field distinguishes them:</span>
<ul>
<li><code>&quot;init&quot;</code>: session metadata for the run. When a <code>SessionStart</code> or <code>Setup</code> hook runs during session startup, its <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sdkhookstartedmessage" class="link">hook lifecycle messages</a> arrive before the <code>init</code> message</li>
<li><code>&quot;compact_boundary&quot;</code>: fires after <a href="#automatic-compaction" class="link">compaction</a></li>
<li><code>&quot;informational&quot;</code>: plain-text status banners from the loop</li>
<li><code>&quot;worker_shutting_down&quot;</code>: the host is exiting or Remote Control disconnected</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">In TypeScript, each subtype other than <code>&quot;init&quot;</code> is its own type in the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sdkmessage" class="link"><code>SDKMessage</code> union</a> rather than a subtype of <code>SDKSystemMessage</code>.</span>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p"><strong><code>AssistantMessage</code>:</strong> emitted for each content block in Claude’s responses, including the final text-only one. Each carries a single content block, such as text or a tool call, and the messages from one response share a message ID.</span>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p"><strong><code>UserMessage</code>:</strong> emitted after each tool execution with the tool result content sent back to Claude. Also emitted for any user inputs you stream mid-loop.</span>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p"><strong><code>StreamEvent</code>:</strong> only emitted when partial messages are enabled. Contains raw API streaming events (text deltas, tool input chunks). See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/streaming-output" class="link">Stream responses</a>.</span>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p"><strong><code>ResultMessage</code>:</strong> marks the end of the agent loop. Contains the final text result, token usage, cost, and session ID. Check the <code>subtype</code> field to determine whether the task succeeded or hit a limit. A small number of trailing system events, such as <code>prompt_suggestion</code>, can arrive after it, so iterate the stream to completion rather than breaking on the result. See <a href="#handle-the-result" class="link">Handle the result</a>.</span>
</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">These five types cover the full agent loop lifecycle. Both SDKs also yield observability events such as rate-limit status and task notifications that are not required to drive the loop. See the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#message-types" class="link">Python message types reference</a> and <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#message-types" class="link">TypeScript message types reference</a> for the complete lists.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="handle-messages" aria-labelledby="_R_1b9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#handle-messages" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Handle messages"></a><span id="_R_1b9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Handle messages</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Which messages you handle depends on what you’re building:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Final results only:</strong> handle <code>ResultMessage</code> to get the output, cost, and whether the task succeeded or hit a limit.</li>
<li><strong>Progress updates:</strong> handle <code>AssistantMessage</code> to see what Claude is doing each turn, including which tools it called.</li>
<li><strong>Live streaming:</strong> enable partial messages (<code>include_partial_messages</code> in Python, <code>includePartialMessages</code> in TypeScript) to get <code>StreamEvent</code> messages in real time. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/streaming-output" class="link">Stream responses in real-time</a>.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">How you check message types depends on the SDK:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Python:</strong> check message types with <code>isinstance()</code> against classes imported from <code>claude_agent_sdk</code> (for example, <code>isinstance(message, ResultMessage)</code>).</li>
<li><strong>TypeScript:</strong> check the <code>type</code> string field (for example, <code>message.type === &quot;result&quot;</code>). <code>AssistantMessage</code> and <code>UserMessage</code> wrap the raw API message in a <code>.message</code> field, so content blocks are at <code>message.message.content</code>, not <code>message.content</code>.</li>
</ul>

<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="tool-execution" aria-labelledby="_R_1n9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#tool-execution" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Tool execution"></a><span id="_R_1n9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Tool execution</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">Tools give your agent the ability to take action. Without tools, Claude can only respond with text. With tools, Claude can read files, run commands, search code, and interact with external services.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="built-in-tools" aria-labelledby="_R_1r9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#built-in-tools" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Built-in tools"></a><span id="_R_1r9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Built-in tools</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">The SDK includes the same tools that power Claude Code:</span>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_1v9deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th style="text-align:left">Category</th><th style="text-align:left">Tools</th><th style="text-align:left">What they do</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style="text-align:left"><strong>File operations</strong></td><td style="text-align:left"><code>Read</code>, <code>Edit</code>, <code>Write</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Read, modify, and create files</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><strong>Search</strong></td><td style="text-align:left"><code>Glob</code>, <code>Grep</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Find files by pattern, search content with regex</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><strong>Execution</strong></td><td style="text-align:left"><code>Bash</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Run shell commands, scripts, git operations</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><strong>Web</strong></td><td style="text-align:left"><code>WebSearch</code>, <code>WebFetch</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Search the web, fetch and parse pages</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><strong>Discovery</strong></td><td style="text-align:left"><code>ToolSearch</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Dynamically find and load tools on-demand instead of preloading all of them</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><strong>Orchestration</strong></td><td style="text-align:left"><code>Agent</code>, <code>Skill</code>, <code>AskUserQuestion</code>, <code>TaskCreate</code>, <code>TaskUpdate</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Spawn subagents, invoke skills, ask the user, track tasks</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">On the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/todo-tracking#model-availability" class="link">models that don’t get the task-tracking tools</a>, Claude Code provides <code>TaskCreate</code> and <code>TaskUpdate</code> only when you opt in.</span>
<span data-as="p">Beyond built-in tools, you can:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Connect external services</strong> with <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/mcp" class="link">MCP servers</a> (databases, browsers, APIs)</li>
<li><strong>Define custom tools</strong> with <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/custom-tools" class="link">custom tool handlers</a></li>
<li><strong>Load project skills</strong> via <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/claude-code-features" class="link">setting sources</a> for reusable workflows</li>
</ul>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="tool-permissions" aria-labelledby="_R_279deehkd74t9_"><a href="#tool-permissions" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Tool permissions"></a><span id="_R_279deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Tool permissions</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Claude determines which tools to call based on the task, but you control whether those calls are allowed to execute. Use these options to auto-approve tools, block tools, and set the permission mode:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong><code>allowed_tools</code> / <code>allowedTools</code></strong> auto-approves listed tools. An agent with <code>[&quot;Read&quot;, &quot;Glob&quot;, &quot;Grep&quot;]</code> in its allowed tools list runs those tools without prompting, with exceptions such as reads from <a href="/docs/en/permissions#network-paths" class="link">network paths</a>. Tools not listed are still available, and calls to them that need approval fall through to the permission mode and <code>canUseTool</code>.</li>
<li><strong><code>disallowed_tools</code> / <code>disallowedTools</code></strong> blocks listed tools, regardless of other settings. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/permissions" class="link">Permissions</a> for the order that rules are checked before a tool runs.</li>
<li><strong><code>permission_mode</code> / <code>permissionMode</code></strong> controls how much human oversight you want. The SDK evaluates the active mode together with your allow and deny rules in a fixed order, described in <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/permissions#how-permissions-are-evaluated" class="link">How permissions are evaluated</a>. See <a href="#permission-mode" class="link">Permission mode</a> for available modes.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">You can also scope individual tools with rules like <code>&quot;Bash(npm *)&quot;</code> to auto-approve specific commands. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/permissions" class="link">Permissions</a> for the full rule syntax.</span>
<span data-as="p">When a tool is denied, Claude receives a rejection message as the tool result and typically attempts a different approach or reports that it couldn’t proceed.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="parallel-tool-execution" aria-labelledby="_R_2h9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#parallel-tool-execution" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Parallel tool execution"></a><span id="_R_2h9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Parallel tool execution</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">When Claude requests multiple tool calls in a single turn, both SDKs can run them concurrently or sequentially depending on the tool. Read-only tools (like <code>Read</code>, <code>Glob</code>, <code>Grep</code>, and MCP tools marked as read-only) can run concurrently. Tools that modify state (like <code>Edit</code>, <code>Write</code>, and <code>Bash</code>) run sequentially to avoid conflicts.</span>
<span data-as="p">Custom tools default to sequential execution. To enable parallel execution for a custom tool, set <code>readOnlyHint</code> in its annotations. Both the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#tool" class="link">TypeScript</a> and <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#tool" class="link">Python</a> SDKs use this field name from the MCP SDK.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="control-how-the-loop-runs" aria-labelledby="_R_2n9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#control-how-the-loop-runs" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Control how the loop runs"></a><span id="_R_2n9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Control how the loop runs</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">You can limit how many turns the loop takes, how much it costs, how deeply Claude reasons, and whether tools require approval before running. All of these are fields on <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#claudeagentoptions" class="link"><code>ClaudeAgentOptions</code></a> (Python) / <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#options" class="link"><code>Options</code></a> (TypeScript).</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="turns-and-budget" aria-labelledby="_R_2r9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#turns-and-budget" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Turns and budget"></a><span id="_R_2r9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Turns and budget</span></h3>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_2t9deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th style="text-align:left">Option</th><th style="text-align:left">What it controls</th><th style="text-align:left">Default</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style="text-align:left">Max turns (<code>max_turns</code> / <code>maxTurns</code>)</td><td style="text-align:left">Maximum number of <a href="#turns-and-messages" class="link">turns</a></td><td style="text-align:left">No limit</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">Max budget (<code>max_budget_usd</code> / <code>maxBudgetUsd</code>)</td><td style="text-align:left">Estimated spend at which the loop stops</td><td style="text-align:left">No limit</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">When either limit is hit, the SDK returns a <code>ResultMessage</code> with a corresponding error subtype (<code>error_max_turns</code> or <code>error_max_budget_usd</code>). See <a href="#handle-the-result" class="link">Handle the result</a> for how to check these subtypes and <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#claudeagentoptions" class="link"><code>ClaudeAgentOptions</code></a> / <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#options" class="link"><code>Options</code></a> for syntax.</span>
<span data-as="p">The budget cap covers <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/subagents" class="link">subagents</a>: their spend counts toward the total. Once spend reaches the cap, spawning another subagent fails with <code>Budget limit reached</code>, and Claude Code stops any background subagents still running. The cap-enforcement behaviors require Claude Code v2.1.217 or later.</span>
<span data-as="p">With <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/streaming-vs-single-mode" class="link">streaming input</a>, a message that is still queued when a turn ends at the max-turns limit stays queued. Claude Code doesn’t add it to that turn’s last model call. It starts a new turn for the message, and the max-turns count starts over for that turn. The budget total keeps accumulating across messages, and once spend reaches <code>maxBudgetUsd</code>, later messages in the same conversation end with the <code>error_max_budget_usd</code> result. A <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/cost-tracking" class="link"><code>/clear</code></a> starts the budget over.</span>
<h4 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="budget-headroom" aria-labelledby="_R_359deehkd74t9_"><a href="#budget-headroom" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Budget headroom"></a><span id="_R_359deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Budget headroom</span></h4>
<span data-as="p">Claude Code compares spend with the <code>max_budget_usd</code> / <code>maxBudgetUsd</code> cap after model responses arrive, because each response’s cost comes from the token usage the API returns with it. The response that reaches the cap still completes and counts toward <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/cost-tracking#get-the-total-cost-of-a-query" class="link"><code>total_cost_usd</code></a>. Spend can therefore pass the cap by up to the cost of that one response, plus anything that subagents still running at that moment spend before they stop. Leave headroom for this when you set the cap.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="effort-level" aria-labelledby="_R_399deehkd74t9_"><a href="#effort-level" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Effort level"></a><span id="_R_399deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Effort level</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">The <code>effort</code> option controls how much reasoning Claude applies. Lower effort levels use fewer tokens per turn and reduce cost. Not all models support the effort parameter. See <a href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/effort" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">Effort</a> for which models support it.</span>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_3d9deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th style="text-align:left">Level</th><th style="text-align:left">Behavior</th><th style="text-align:left">Good for</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>&quot;low&quot;</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Minimal reasoning, fast responses</td><td style="text-align:left">File lookups, listing directories</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>&quot;medium&quot;</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Balanced reasoning</td><td style="text-align:left">Routine edits, standard tasks</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>&quot;high&quot;</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Thorough analysis</td><td style="text-align:left">Refactors, debugging</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>&quot;xhigh&quot;</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Extended reasoning depth</td><td style="text-align:left">Coding and agentic tasks on the <a href="/docs/en/model-config#adjust-effort-level" class="link">models that support it</a></td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>&quot;max&quot;</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Maximum reasoning depth</td><td style="text-align:left">Multi-step problems requiring deep analysis</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">If you don’t set <code>effort</code>, Claude Code resolves the effort level itself, in the order <a href="/docs/en/model-config#adjust-effort-level" class="link">Adjust effort level</a> describes.</span>
<div role="note" aria-label="Note" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-blue-200 bg-blue-50 dark:border-blue-900 dark:bg-blue-600/20" data-callout-type="note"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="size-4 text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutNote"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p"><code>effort</code> trades latency and token cost for reasoning depth within each response. <a href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/extended-thinking" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">Extended thinking</a> produces <code>thinking</code> blocks in the output, and the <code>display</code> field on <code>ThinkingConfig</code> for <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#thinkingconfig" class="link">Python</a> or <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#thinkingconfig" class="link">TypeScript</a> controls whether you receive their text.</span></div></div>
<span data-as="p">Use lower effort for agents doing simple, well-scoped tasks, such as listing files or running a single grep, to reduce cost and latency. Set <code>effort</code> in the top-level <code>query()</code> options for the <a href="/docs/en/sub-agents#choose-an-effort-level" class="link">session</a>, or per subagent with the <code>effort</code> field on <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/subagents#agentdefinition-configuration" class="link"><code>AgentDefinition</code></a> to override the session level.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="permission-mode" aria-labelledby="_R_3l9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#permission-mode" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Permission mode"></a><span id="_R_3l9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Permission mode</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">The permission mode option (<code>permission_mode</code> in Python, <code>permissionMode</code> in TypeScript) controls whether the agent asks for approval before using tools:</span>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_3p9deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th style="text-align:left">Mode</th><th style="text-align:left">Behavior</th><th style="text-align:left">Use case</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>&quot;default&quot;</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Tool calls that need approval and aren’t covered by allow rules trigger your <code>canUseTool</code> callback; no callback means deny</td><td style="text-align:left">Interactive applications with a custom approval callback</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>&quot;acceptEdits&quot;</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Auto-approves file edits and common filesystem commands (<code>mkdir</code>, <code>touch</code>, <code>mv</code>, <code>cp</code>, etc.) inside your <a href="/docs/en/permissions#working-directories" class="link">working directories</a>; other Bash commands follow default rules</td><td style="text-align:left">You trust Claude’s edits and want faster iteration, such as during prototyping or when working in an isolated directory</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>&quot;plan&quot;</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Claude explores and plans. Edits made with Claude’s file tools go to your <code>canUseTool</code> callback even when an allow rule matches them</td><td style="text-align:left">You want Claude to produce a plan first, such as during code review or before a large change</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>&quot;dontAsk&quot;</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Never prompts. Tools pre-approved by <a href="/docs/en/settings-reference#permission-settings" class="link">permission rules</a> run, and so do calls that need no approval in <code>default</code> mode, such as file reads inside your working directories. Every call that would otherwise prompt is denied, though a <a href="/docs/en/plugins/mods/overview" class="link">mod</a> you installed can still approve <a href="/docs/en/permissions#precedence-among-rules-hooks-and-mods" class="link">some of those calls</a>. A few calls, such as <a href="/docs/en/permissions#network-paths" class="link">reads from network paths</a>, are denied even if you’ve allowed them. For the list, see <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/permissions#don%E2%80%99t-ask-mode-dontask" class="link">Don’t ask mode</a></td><td style="text-align:left">Headless agents with nobody to approve calls</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>&quot;auto&quot;</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Uses a model classifier to review actions such as shell commands and network requests, allowing or blocking each one it reviews. See <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#eliminate-prompts-with-auto-mode" class="link">Auto mode</a> for availability and the decision order</td><td style="text-align:left">Autonomous agents that still want safety guardrails on tool use</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>&quot;bypassPermissions&quot;</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Runs tool calls without asking for approval, including calls to tools you didn’t list in <code>allowedTools</code>. A few calls still go to your <code>canUseTool</code> callback, listed under <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/permissions#bypass-permissions-mode-bypasspermissions" class="link">Bypass permissions mode</a>. In the TypeScript SDK, also requires <code>allowDangerouslySkipPermissions: true</code> in <code>options</code>. Can’t be used when running as root on Unix. Use only in isolated environments where the agent’s actions can’t affect systems you care about</td><td style="text-align:left">CI, containers, or other isolated environments</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">For interactive applications, use <code>&quot;default&quot;</code> with a tool approval callback to surface approval prompts. For agents that edit files on a dev machine, use <code>&quot;acceptEdits&quot;</code>. Reserve <code>&quot;bypassPermissions&quot;</code> for CI, containers, or other isolated environments. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/permissions" class="link">Permissions</a> for full details.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="model" aria-labelledby="_R_3t9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#model" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Model"></a><span id="_R_3t9deehkd74t9_" class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Model</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Set the <code>model</code> option to choose which model runs the session. For more information, see <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/configuration#choose-a-model" class="link">Choose a model</a>.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="the-context-window" aria-labelledby="_R_419deehkd74t9_"><a href="#the-context-window" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: The context window"></a><span id="_R_419deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">The context window</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">The context window is the total amount of information available to Claude during a session. It does not reset between turns within a session. Everything accumulates: the system prompt, tool definitions, conversation history, tool inputs, and tool outputs. Content that stays the same across turns (system prompt, tool definitions, CLAUDE.md) is automatically <a href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">prompt cached</a>, which reduces cost and latency for repeated prefixes. For how a custom system prompt or <code>append</code> text affects cache reuse across sessions, see <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/modifying-system-prompts#improve-prompt-caching-across-users-and-machines" class="link">Modifying system prompts</a>.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="what-consumes-context" aria-labelledby="_R_459deehkd74t9_"><a href="#what-consumes-context" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: What consumes context"></a><span id="_R_459deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">What consumes context</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Here’s how each component affects context in the SDK:</span>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_499deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th style="text-align:left">Source</th><th style="text-align:left">When it loads</th><th style="text-align:left">Impact</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style="text-align:left"><strong>System prompt</strong></td><td style="text-align:left">Every request</td><td style="text-align:left">Small fixed cost, always present</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><strong>CLAUDE.md files</strong></td><td style="text-align:left">Session start, via <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/claude-code-features" class="link"><code>settingSources</code></a></td><td style="text-align:left">Full content in every request (but prompt-cached)</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><strong>Tool definitions</strong></td><td style="text-align:left">Every request for core built-in tools such as Bash, Read, and Edit; on demand for MCP tools and other built-in tools, which <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/mcp#mcp-tool-search" class="link">tool search</a> defers by default</td><td style="text-align:left">Every schema loads upfront in the cases under <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/tool-search#configure-tool-search" class="link">Configure tool search</a></td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><strong>Conversation history</strong></td><td style="text-align:left">Accumulates over turns</td><td style="text-align:left">Grows with each turn: prompts, responses, tool inputs, tool outputs</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><strong>Skill descriptions</strong></td><td style="text-align:left">Session start, via setting sources</td><td style="text-align:left">Short summaries; full content loads only when invoked</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">Large tool outputs consume significant context. Reading a big file or running a command with verbose output can use thousands of tokens in a single turn. Context accumulates across turns, so longer sessions with many tool calls build up significantly more context than short ones.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="automatic-compaction" aria-labelledby="_R_4d9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#automatic-compaction" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Automatic compaction"></a><span id="_R_4d9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Automatic compaction</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">When the context window approaches its limit, the SDK automatically compacts the conversation: it summarizes older history to free space, keeping your most recent exchanges and key decisions intact. The SDK emits a message with <code>type: &quot;system&quot;</code> and <code>subtype: &quot;compact_boundary&quot;</code> in the stream when this happens (in Python this is a <code>SystemMessage</code>; in TypeScript it is a separate <code>SDKCompactBoundaryMessage</code> type).</span>
<span data-as="p">Compaction replaces older messages with a summary, so specific instructions from early in the conversation may not be preserved. Persistent rules belong in CLAUDE.md (loaded via <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/claude-code-features" class="link"><code>settingSources</code></a>) rather than in the initial prompt, because CLAUDE.md content is re-injected on every request.</span>
<span data-as="p">You can customize compaction behavior in several ways:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Summarization instructions in CLAUDE.md:</strong> The compactor reads your CLAUDE.md like any other context, so you can include a section telling it what to preserve when summarizing. The compactor matches on intent, so the section header is free-form.</li>
<li><strong><code>PreCompact</code> hook:</strong> Run custom logic before compaction occurs, for example to archive the full transcript. The hook receives a <code>trigger</code> field (<code>manual</code> or <code>auto</code>). See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/hooks" class="link">hooks</a>.</li>
<li><strong>Manual compaction:</strong> Send <code>/compact</code> as a prompt string to trigger compaction on demand. Commands sent this way are ordinary SDK inputs. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/skills#dispatch-commands-by-name" class="link">dispatch commands by name</a>.</li>
</ul>

<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">When summarizing this conversation, always preserve:</span></span>

</code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true" style="--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E"></div></div></div></details>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="keep-context-efficient" aria-labelledby="_R_4p9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#keep-context-efficient" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Keep context efficient"></a><span id="_R_4p9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Keep context efficient</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">A few strategies for long-running agents:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Use subagents for subtasks.</strong> A subagent returns only its final response, so your main agent’s context grows by a summary, not by the subtask’s transcript.</li>
<li><strong>Be selective with tools.</strong> Every tool definition takes context space. Use the <code>tools</code> field on <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/subagents#agentdefinition-configuration" class="link"><code>AgentDefinition</code></a> to scope subagents to the minimum set they need.</li>
<li><strong>Watch MCP server costs.</strong> <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/mcp#mcp-tool-search" class="link">MCP tool search</a> defers MCP tool schemas by default and loads them on demand. When tool search is off or has fallen back to upfront loading, each MCP server adds all its tool schemas to every request, so a few servers with many tools can consume significant context before the agent does any work. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/tool-search#configure-tool-search" class="link">Configure tool search</a> for the configurations where the fallback applies.</li>
<li><strong>Use lower effort for routine tasks.</strong> Set <a href="#effort-level" class="link">effort</a> to <code>&quot;low&quot;</code> for agents that only need to read files or list directories. This reduces token usage and cost.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">For a detailed breakdown of per-feature context costs, see <a href="/docs/en/features-overview#understand-context-costs" class="link">Understand context costs</a>.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="sessions-and-continuity" aria-labelledby="_R_519deehkd74t9_"><a href="#sessions-and-continuity" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Sessions and continuity"></a><span id="_R_519deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Sessions and continuity</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">Each interaction with the SDK creates or continues a session. Capture the session ID from <code>ResultMessage.session_id</code> (available in both SDKs) to resume later. The TypeScript SDK also exposes it as a direct field on the init <code>SystemMessage</code>; in Python it’s nested in <code>SystemMessage.data</code>.</span>
<span data-as="p">When you resume, the full context from previous turns is restored: files that were read, analysis that was performed, and actions that were taken. You can also fork a session to branch into a different approach without modifying the original.</span>
<span data-as="p">See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/sessions" class="link">Session management</a> for the full guide on resume, continue, and fork patterns. To resume sessions across stateless containers or serverless hosts, pass a <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/session-storage" class="link"><code>session_store</code> / <code>sessionStore</code> adapter</a> so the SDK mirrors transcripts to your own backend and another host can resume them. The Claude Code subprocess still writes to local disk first. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/session-storage#dual-write-architecture" class="link">Dual-write architecture</a> for which copy outlives a fresh session versus a run resumed from the store, and how to keep the local copy ephemeral.</span>
<div role="note" aria-label="Note" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-blue-200 bg-blue-50 dark:border-blue-900 dark:bg-blue-600/20" data-callout-type="note"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="size-4 text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutNote"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">In Python, <code>ClaudeSDKClient</code> handles session IDs automatically across multiple calls. See the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#choosing-between-query-and-claudesdkclient" class="link">Python SDK reference</a> for details.</span></div></div>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="handle-the-result" aria-labelledby="_R_5b9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#handle-the-result" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Handle the result"></a><span id="_R_5b9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Handle the result</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">When the loop ends, the <code>ResultMessage</code> tells you what happened and gives you the output. The <code>subtype</code> field (available in both SDKs) is the primary way to check termination state.</span>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_5f9deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th style="text-align:left">Result subtype</th><th style="text-align:left">What happened</th><th style="text-align:center"><code>result</code> field available?</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>success</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Claude finished the task normally</td><td style="text-align:center">Yes</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>error_max_turns</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Hit the <code>maxTurns</code> limit before finishing</td><td style="text-align:center">No</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>error_max_budget_usd</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Hit the <code>maxBudgetUsd</code> limit before finishing</td><td style="text-align:center">No</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>error_during_execution</code></td><td style="text-align:left">An error interrupted the loop (for example, a cancelled request)</td><td style="text-align:center">No</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>error_max_structured_output_retries</code></td><td style="text-align:left">No valid structured output was produced within the configured retry limit: every attempt failed validation, or a model fallback retracted the completed output with no successful retry</td><td style="text-align:center">No</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">The <code>result</code> field holds the final text output and is only present on the <code>success</code> variant, so always check the subtype before reading it.</span>
<span data-as="p">All result subtypes carry <code>total_cost_usd</code>, <code>usage</code>, <code>num_turns</code>, and <code>session_id</code> so you can track cost and resume even after errors. Guard for these cases:</span>
<ul>
<li>After a session crash, the final result is an <code>error_during_execution</code> whose cost fields may be zeroed and whose <code>stop_reason</code> is <code>null</code>, and the process exits after emitting it. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/cost-tracking#recover-totals-after-a-session-crash" class="link">Recover totals after a session crash</a>.</li>
<li>In Python, <code>total_cost_usd</code>, <code>usage</code>, and <code>model_usage</code> are typed as optional, so check that they aren’t <code>None</code> before you read them.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">The <code>usage</code> field covers only the main agent loop. Use <code>modelUsage</code>, or <code>model_usage</code> in Python, for whole-tree token and cost accounting. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/cost-tracking" class="link">Tracking costs and usage</a> for details on interpreting the <code>usage</code> fields.</span>
<div role="note" aria-label="Note" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-blue-200 bg-blue-50 dark:border-blue-900 dark:bg-blue-600/20" data-callout-type="note"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="size-4 text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutNote"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">When a query ends on an error result:</span><ul>
<li>A single-shot <code>query()</code> call yields the final result message, then raises an error that includes the failure text, such as <code>Reached maximum number of turns</code>. The raise is intentional. Wrap the loop in a try block if your code needs to continue past it. The underlying Claude Code process also exits with a nonzero code.</li>
<li>A streaming input session stays alive, and you can keep sending messages, except after a session crash, which emits a final <code>error_during_execution</code> result and exits the process.</li>
<li>In TypeScript, the loop over <code>query()</code> in a streaming input session throws when the last response ends in an error result. Wrap the loop in a try block if your code needs to continue past it.</li>
</ul></div></div>
<span data-as="p">The result also includes a <code>stop_reason</code> field (<code>string | null</code> in TypeScript, <code>str | None</code> in Python) indicating why the model stopped generating on its final turn. Common values are <code>end_turn</code> (model finished normally), <code>max_tokens</code> (hit the output token limit), and <code>refusal</code> (the model declined the request). On error results that the loop produced, <code>stop_reason</code> carries the value from the last assistant response before the loop ended; the result Claude Code synthesizes after a session crash carries <code>null</code>.</span>
<span data-as="p">To detect refusals, check <code>stop_reason === &quot;refusal&quot;</code> (TypeScript) or <code>stop_reason == &quot;refusal&quot;</code> (Python). See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sdkresultmessage" class="link"><code>SDKResultMessage</code></a> (TypeScript) or <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#resultmessage" class="link"><code>ResultMessage</code></a> (Python) for the full type.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="hooks" aria-labelledby="_R_5v9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#hooks" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Hooks"></a><span id="_R_5v9deehkd74t9_" class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Hooks</span></h2>
<span data-as="p"><a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/hooks" class="link">Hooks</a> are callbacks that fire at specific points in the loop: before a tool runs, after it returns, when the agent finishes, and so on. Some commonly used hooks are:</span>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_639deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th style="text-align:left">Hook</th><th style="text-align:left">When it fires</th><th style="text-align:left">Common uses</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>PreToolUse</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Before a tool executes</td><td style="text-align:left">Validate inputs, block dangerous commands</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>PostToolUse</code></td><td style="text-align:left">After a tool returns</td><td style="text-align:left">Audit outputs, trigger side effects</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>UserPromptSubmit</code></td><td style="text-align:left">When a prompt is sent</td><td style="text-align:left">Inject additional context into prompts</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>Stop</code></td><td style="text-align:left">When the agent finishes</td><td style="text-align:left">Validate the result, save session state</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>SubagentStart</code> / <code>SubagentStop</code></td><td style="text-align:left">When a subagent spawns or completes</td><td style="text-align:left">Track and aggregate parallel task results</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code>PreCompact</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Before context compaction</td><td style="text-align:left">Archive full transcript before summarizing</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">Hooks run in your application process, not inside the agent’s context window, so they don’t consume context. Hooks can also short-circuit the loop: a <code>PreToolUse</code> hook that rejects a tool call prevents it from executing, and Claude receives the rejection message instead.</span>
<span data-as="p">Both SDKs support all the events above. The TypeScript SDK includes additional events that Python does not yet support. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/hooks" class="link">Control execution with hooks</a> for the complete event list, per-SDK availability, and the full callback API.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="put-it-all-together" aria-labelledby="_R_699deehkd74t9_"><a href="#put-it-all-together" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Put it all together"></a><span id="_R_699deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Put it all together</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">This example combines the key concepts from this page into a single agent that fixes failing tests. It configures the agent with allowed tools (auto-approved so the agent runs autonomously), project settings, and safety limits on turns and reasoning effort. As the loop runs, it captures the session ID for potential resumption, handles the final result, and prints the total cost.</span>
<span data-as="p">Because a single-shot <code>query()</code> call raises after yielding an error result, the loop is wrapped in a try block so the script exits cleanly when a limit is hit.</span>

<span data-as="p">When the agent finishes successfully, the example prints a <code>Done:</code> line with the agent’s summary of the fix, then a line like <code>Cost: $0.0312</code>.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="next-steps" aria-labelledby="_R_6j9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#next-steps" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Next steps"></a><span id="_R_6j9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Next steps</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">Now that you understand the loop, here’s where to go depending on what you’re building:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Haven’t run an agent yet?</strong> Start with the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/quickstart" class="link">quickstart</a> to get the SDK installed and see a full example running end to end.</li>
<li><strong>Ready to hook into your project?</strong> <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/claude-code-features" class="link">Load CLAUDE.md, skills, and filesystem hooks</a> so the agent follows your project conventions automatically.</li>
<li><strong>Building an interactive UI?</strong> Enable <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/streaming-output" class="link">streaming</a> to show live text and tool calls as the loop runs.</li>
<li><strong>Need tighter control over what the agent can do?</strong> Lock down tool access with <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/permissions" class="link">permissions</a>, and use <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/hooks" class="link">hooks</a> to audit, block, or transform tool calls before they execute.</li>
<li><strong>Running long or expensive tasks?</strong> Offload isolated work to <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/subagents" class="link">subagents</a> to keep your main context lean.</li>
<li><strong>Deploying as a service?</strong> See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/hosting" class="link">Hosting the Agent SDK</a> for container and serverless guidance, and <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/session-storage" class="link">Session storage</a> to persist sessions to your own backend.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">For the broader conceptual picture of the agentic loop (not SDK-specific), see <a href="/docs/en/how-claude-code-works" class="link">How Claude Code works</a>. For a practical guide to designing loops in Claude Code, from turn-based to goal-based and proactive loops, see <a href="https://claude.com/blog/getting-started-with-loops" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">Loop engineering: getting started with loops</a> on the blog.</span></div><nav id="pagination" data-layout-item="pagination" aria-label="Pagination" class="px-0.5 flex 2500w`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ol,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Receive prompt.`}),` Claude receives your prompt, along with the system prompt, tool definitions, and conversation history. The SDK yields a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#message-types`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SystemMessage`})}),` with subtype `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`\"init\"`}),` containing session metadata.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Evaluate and respond.`}),` Claude evaluates the current state and determines how to proceed. It may respond with text, request one or more tool calls, or both. The SDK yields one or more `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#message-types`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`AssistantMessage`})}),` objects, one for each content block, such as a text block or a tool call request.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Execute tools.`}),` The SDK runs each requested tool and collects the results. Each set of tool results feeds back to Claude for the next decision. You can use `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/hooks`,children:`hooks`}),` to intercept, modify, or block tool calls before they run.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Repeat.`}),` Steps 2 and 3 repeat as a cycle. Claude continues calling tools and processing results until it produces a response with no tool calls.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Return result.`}),` The SDK yields a final `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#message-types`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`AssistantMessage`})}),` with the text response (no tool calls), followed by a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#message-types`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ResultMessage`})}),` with the final text, token usage, cost, and session ID.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`A quick question such as “what files are here?” might take two or three `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#turns-and-messages`,children:`turns`}),`: one or two calling `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Glob`}),`, then one responding with the results. A complex task such as “refactor the auth module and update the tests” can chain dozens of tool calls across many turns as Claude adjusts after each result.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`2`,id:`turns-and-messages`,children:`Turns and messages`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`A turn is one pass through the loop: Claude produces output, and if it includes tool calls, the SDK executes them and the results feed back to Claude automatically. This happens without yielding control back to your code. Turns continue until Claude produces output with no tool calls, at which point the loop ends and the final result is delivered.`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Consider what a full session might look like for the prompt “Fix the failing tests in auth.ts”.`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`First, the SDK sends your prompt to Claude and yields a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#message-types`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SystemMessage`})}),` with the session metadata. Then the loop begins:`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ol,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Turn 1:`}),` Claude calls `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Bash`}),` to run `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`npm test`}),`. The SDK yields an `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#message-types`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`AssistantMessage`})}),` with the tool call, executes the command, then yields a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#message-types`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`UserMessage`})}),` with the output (three failures).`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Turn 2:`}),` Claude calls `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Read`}),` on `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`auth.ts`}),` and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`auth.test.ts`}),`. The SDK yields an `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`AssistantMessage`}),` for each call and returns the file contents.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Turn 3:`}),` Claude calls `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Edit`}),` to fix `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`auth.ts`}),`, then calls `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Bash`}),` to re-run `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`npm test`}),`. All three tests pass. The SDK yields an `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`AssistantMessage`}),` for each call.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Final turn:`}),` Claude produces a text-only response with no tool calls: “Fixed the auth bug, all three tests pass now.” The SDK yields a final `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`AssistantMessage`}),` with this text, then a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#message-types`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ResultMessage`})}),` with the same text plus cost and usage.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`That was four turns: three with tool calls, one final text-only response.`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`You can cap the loop with `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`max_turns`}),` / `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`maxTurns`}),`. Count each turn with tool calls, plus one for the final text-only response. Without that extra turn, the run ends with a `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ResultMessage`}),` of subtype `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`error_max_turns`}),` and no final response. For example, `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`max_turns=2`}),` in the loop above would have stopped before the edit step. You can also use `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`max_budget_usd`}),` / `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`maxBudgetUsd`}),` to cap turns based on a spend threshold.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Without limits, the loop runs until Claude finishes on its own, which is fine for well-scoped tasks but can run long on open-ended prompts (“improve this codebase”). Setting a budget is a good default for production agents. See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#turns-and-budget`,children:`Turns and budget`}),` below for the option reference.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`2`,id:`message-types`,children:`Message types`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`As the loop runs, the SDK yields a stream of messages. Each message carries a type that tells you what stage of the loop it came from. The five core types are:`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[_jsxs(_components.strong,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SystemMessage`}),`:`]}),` session lifecycle events. The `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`subtype`}),` field distinguishes them:`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`\"init\"`}),`: session metadata for the run. When a `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SessionStart`}),` or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Setup`}),` hook runs during session startup, its `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sdkhookstartedmessage`,children:`hook lifecycle messages`}),` arrive before the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`init`}),` message`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`\"compact_boundary\"`}),`: fires after `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#automatic-compaction`,children:`compaction`})]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`\"informational\"`}),`: plain-text status banners from the loop`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`\"worker_shutting_down\"`}),`: the host is exiting or Remote Control disconnected`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`In TypeScript, each subtype other than `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`\"init\"`}),` is its own type in the `,_jsxs(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sdkmessage`,children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SDKMessage`}),` union`]}),` rather than a subtype of `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SDKSystemMessage`}),`.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[_jsxs(_components.strong,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`AssistantMessage`}),`:`]}),` emitted for each content block in Claude’s responses, including the final text-only one. Each carries a single content block, such as text or a tool call, and the messages from one response share a message ID.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[_jsxs(_components.strong,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`UserMessage`}),`:`]}),` emitted after each tool execution with the tool result content sent back to Claude. Also emitted for any user inputs you stream mid-loop.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[_jsxs(_components.strong,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`StreamEvent`}),`:`]}),` only emitted when partial messages are enabled. Contains raw API streaming events (text deltas, tool input chunks). See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/streaming-output`,children:`Stream responses`}),`.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[_jsxs(_components.strong,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ResultMessage`}),`:`]}),` marks the end of the agent loop. Contains the final text result, token usage, cost, and session ID. Check the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`subtype`}),` field to determine whether the task succeeded or hit a limit. A small number of trailing system events, such as `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`prompt_suggestion`}),`, can arrive after it, so iterate the stream to completion rather than breaking on the result. See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#handle-the-result`,children:`Handle the result`}),`.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`These five types cover the full agent loop lifecycle. Both SDKs also yield observability events such as rate-limit status and task notifications that are not required to drive the loop. See the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/python#message-types`,children:`Python message types reference`}),` and `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/typescript#message-types`,children:`TypeScript message types reference`}),` for the complete lists.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`handle-messages`,children:`Handle messages`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Which messages you handle depends on what you’re building:`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Final results only:`}),` handle `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ResultMessage`}),` to get the output, cost, and whether the task succeeded or hit a limit.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Progress updates:`}),` handle `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`AssistantMessage`}),` to see what Claude is doing each turn, including which tools it called.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Live streaming:`}),` enable partial messages (`,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`include_partial_messages`}),` in Python, `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`includePartialMessages`}),` in TypeScript) to get `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`StreamEvent`}),` messages in real time. See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/streaming-output`,children:`Stream responses in real-time`}),`.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`How you check message types depends on the Read-only tools (like `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Read`}),`, `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Glob`}),`, `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Grep`}),`, and MCP tools marked as read-only) can run concurrently. Tools that modify state (like `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Edit`}),`, `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Write`}),`, and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Bash`}),`) run sequentially to avoid conflicts.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Custom tools default to sequential execution. To enable parallel execution for a custom tool, set `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`readOnlyHint`}),` in its annotations. Both the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/typescript#tool`,children:`TypeScript`}),` and `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/python#tool`,children:`Python`}),` SDKs use this field name from the MCP SDK.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`2`,id:`control-how-the-loop-runs`,children:`Control how the loop runs`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`You can limit how many turns the loop takes, how much it costs, how deeply Claude reasons, and whether tools require approval before running. All of these are fields on `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/python#claudeagentoptions`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ClaudeAgentOptions`})}),` (Python) / `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/typescript#options`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Options`})}),` (TypeScript).`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`turns-and-budget`,children:`Turns and budget`}),`\n`,_jsxs(Table,{children:[_jsx(_components.thead,{children:_jsxs(_components.tr,{children:[_jsx(_components.th,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:`Option`}),_jsx(_components.th,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:`What it controls`}),_jsx(_components.th,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:`Default`})]})}),_jsxs(_components.tbody,{children:[_jsxs(_components.tr,{children:[_jsxs(_components.td,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:[`Max turns (`,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`max_turns`}),` / `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`maxTurns`}),`)`]}),_jsxs(_components.td,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:[`Maximum number of `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#turns-and-messages`,children:`turns`})]}),_jsx(_components.td,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:`No limit`})]}),_jsxs(_components.tr,{children:[_jsxs(_components.td,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:[`Max budget (`,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`max_budget_usd`}),` / `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`maxBudgetUsd`}),`)`]}),_jsx(_components.td,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:`Estimated spend at which the loop stops`}),_jsx(_components.td,{style:{textAlign:`left`},children:`No limit`})]})]})]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`When either limit is hit, the SDK returns a `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ResultMessage`}),` with a corresponding error subtype (`,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`error_max_turns`}),` or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`error_max_budget_usd`}),`). See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#handle-the-result`,children:`Handle the result`}),` for how to check these subtypes and `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/python#claudeagentoptions`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ClaudeAgentOptions`})}),` / `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/typescript#options`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Options`})}),` for syntax.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The budget cap covers `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/subagents`,children:`subagents`}),`: their spend counts toward the total. Once spend reaches the cap, spawning another subagent fails with `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Budget limit reached`}),`, and Claude Code stops any background subagents still running. The cap-enforcement behaviors require Claude Code v2.1.217 or later.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`With `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/streaming-vs-single-mode`,children:`streaming input`}),`, a message that is still queued when a turn ends at the max-turns limit stays queued. Claude Code doesn’t add it to that turn’s last model call. It starts a new turn for the message, and the max-turns count starts over for that turn. The budget total keeps accumulating across messages, and once spend reaches `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`maxBudgetUsd`}),`, later messages in the same conversation end with the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`error_max_budget_usd`}),` result. A `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/cost-tracking`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/clear`})}),` starts the budget over.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`4`,id:`budget-headroom`,children:`Budget headroom`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Claude Code compares spend with the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`max_budget_usd`}),` / `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`maxBudgetUsd`}),` cap after model responses arrive, because each response’s cost comes from the token usage the API returns with it. The response that reaches the cap still completes and counts toward `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/cost-tracking#get-the-total-cost-of-a-query`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`total_cost_usd`})}),`. Spend can therefore pass the cap by up to the cost of that one response, plus anything that subagents still running at that moment spend before they stop. Leave headroom for this when you set the cap.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`effort-level`,children:`Effort level`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`effort`}),` option controls how much reasoning Claude applies.
